The remaining big wins would need fewer weight                    205 +  bytes (quantization) or more cores, and the rules forbid both. Further tuning attempts (tile                   206 +  width, unroll depth) were within noise.                                                                         207  - **Summation order**: each matvec output is summed in the same order as run.c. Numba emits no  76 -  FMA here (no `contract` flag), while the build that produced the fixtures may have fused                       208 +  FMA (no `contract` flag), while the build that produced the fixtures may have fused                            209    multiply-adds. The differences are ~1e-5 in the logits.  78 -- **Load-time transpose**: weights sit in memory once, in transposed form, plus an extra 98 MB                    79 -  transposed classifier copy because the classifier is shared with the embedding. Peak memory                      80 -  during load is about 1 GB.                                                                                     210 +- **Memory**: the tiled weights (438 MB) and the tiled classifier copy (98 MB, because it is                     211 +  shared with the 98 MB embedding) stay resident. The checkpoint is mapped read-only during load.                212 +  Load time depends heavily on OS memory pressure: the first load in a fresh process after                       213 +  recompiling or under pressure was seen at up to ~1.6 s.                                                         214 +- **Long contexts**: attention and the KV-cache stores grow with position. They are 3% at the dev                215 +  workloads' ≤82 positions, but larger near seq_len = 1024. That range was only exercised by                     216 +  `edge.py` (~10.8 s for 1016 forced + 9 new positions), not profiled in detail.                                 217 +- **Startup** (~1.3–1.6 s): ~0.55 s is importing numpy+numba, ~0.4 s is loading and tiling, and                  218 +  ~0.1 s is a warm-up forward. There is no further easy cut within the rules.                                     219  - **Numba on-disk cache** (`cache=True`) writes compiled code to `__pycache__/` beside `run.py`. 220    Without it (e.g. a read-only dir with no fallback), the first start compiles for a few seconds. 221    Before `READY`, a single warm-up forward runs and the KV cache is reset afterwards....  226    not range-checked (bounds checks are off in the kernels). 227  - The tokenizer and protocol handling are plain Python (not compute); only the arithmetic is 228    compiled.  91 -- Throughput on this machine moved a lot between runs (roughly 62–104 tok/s for the same command),                92 -  apparently from other load, so the speed figures above are rough.                                               229 +- **Measurement noise**: the machine was shared and memory-pressured throughout. Identical                       230 +  commands varied by ±15–30% between runs, so treat the figures as medians under that load.                     ╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌╌ Do you want to overwrite NOTES.md?❯1. Tile widths 64/256 and 94 -  an 8-row unroll were within noise of T=128 with 4 rows (`bench_tile3.py`). Untiled classifier         95 -  tiles of 256–3200 columns were ≤1.9 ms (`bench_cls*.py`).                                             94 +  an 8-row unroll were within noise of T=128 with 4 rows (`bench_tile3.py`). With a 2-row                  95 +  kernel, classifier tiles of 256–1280 columns reached ~1.9 ms (min), and wider tiles got worse         96 +  (3200 columns: 2.5 ms) (`bench_cls*.py`).                                                                 97   98  **After** (`scratch/profile_after.txt`): ~7.7–8.2 ms/token, 55–57 GB/s effective.     99  ✶Fluttering…✽17s · ↓ 54.2k tokens)Profiling. I used only the fixtures.json workloads that check.py runs. A forward pass took 10.0 ms per token andislimited by memory bandwidth: 438 MB of weights are read per token, and one M1 core streams about 56 GB/s at best.Thefinal classifier was the main problem: 30% of the time at only 32 GB/s, because its 128 KB output vector kept fallingout of the L1 cache. The other matrix multiplies ran at 51–55 GB/s and attention took 4%.Changes:-Tiled weights:everyweightmatrixisstoredinblocksof128outputcolumns.Eachblockstreamscontiguouslyandits outputs stay in L1, which raised all matrix multiplies to about58–60GB/s.Eachoutputisstillsummedinthesame order as run.c.-Combined matrix calls:theq/k/vmatricesandthew1/w3matricesareeachhandledinonecallperlayer.-Attention: unrolled by two, keeping the same order of additions (0.44 → 0.25 ms per token).-RoPE: the cos/sin values are computed once per position instead of once per layer. The values are bit-identical.-Loading: the checkpoint is memory-mapped and copied into the tiled layout with a faster loop.Results(mediansonashared,memory-pressuredM1,soexpect±15–30%run-to-runvariation):┌──────────────────────────────────┬──────────┬─────────┐ │                            │  Befoe  │  Aftr  │├──────────────────────────────────┼──────────┼─────────┤ │ Forward pass, mspertoken│8.9–10.0│7.7–8.3│  ├──────────────────────────────────┼──────────┼─────────┤│GENERATE 64 tokens/s │68–90│94–114│├──────────────────────────────────┼──────────┼─────────┤│Full check-equivalent session    │ 7.5 s    │ 5.4 s   │├──────────────────────────────────┼──────────┼─────────┤│Startup to READY                 │ 1.7 s    │ 1.45 s  │├──────────────────────────────────┼──────────┼─────────┤│~1024-position long-context test │ 15.8 s   │ 10.8 s  │└──────────────────────────────────┴──────────┴─────────┘Tests beyond check.py:-Old vs new forward pass:bitwiseidenticalonallthreedevsequences. - Synthetic checkpoints:cover cases thesuppliedmodelneverhits(dimensionsthatneedpaddingtowholetiles,fewer    key/value heads than query heads, unshared classifier weights, running to seq_len).
